# Concrete Calculator — https://calculatorst.com/concrete-calculator/
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Slug: concrete-calculator
Title: Concrete Calculator — Volume, Weight & Cost
H1: Concrete Calculator
Category: Concrete / Cluster: concrete
Description: Calculate concrete volume, weight and cost for any slab, footing or wall. Supports cubic yards, bags and ready-mix.
URL: https://calculatorst.com/concrete-calculator/
Canonical: https://calculatorst.com/concrete-calculator/
Visual Variant: concrete
Breadcrumbs: Home > Construction > Concrete > Concrete Calculator

[1] WHAT IS THIS CALCULATOR?
The Concrete Calculator — Estimates concrete volume, weight and ready-mix versus bag count for US slabs, footings and walls.

[2] WHAT DOES IT CALCULATE?
- Concrete volume in cubic yards and cubic feet
- Ready-mix loads vs 40/60/80-lb bags
- Weight and cost with waste

[3] HOW TO USE
1. Measure Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in — use feet for length/width and inches for thickness where typical.
2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Volume = Length x Width x Depth
Variables:
  - L: Length in feet
  - W: Width in feet
  - D: Depth in feet

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in
Steps:
  - Convert depth: 4 in /12 = 0.333 ft
  - Cubic feet: 20 x 10 x 0.333 = 66.67 ft3
  - Cubic yards: 66.67 /27 = 2.47 yd3
  - With 10% waste: 2.72 yd3

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Concrete — 4000 psi typical for driveways, 3000 psi for patios. Ordered by cubic yard (27 ft³) or bags (0.60 ft³ per 80-lb). Keep slump 4 in for slabs.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Concrete — 4000 psi typical for driveways, 3000 psi for patios. Ordered by cubic yard (27 ft³) or bags (0.60 ft³ per 80-lb). Keep slump 4 in for slabs.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 5–10% for spillage and uneven subgrade — 10% is safe for hand-screeded forms.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Order ready-mix over ~1 yd³ (45 bags) — faster and cheaper
- Keep subgrade compacted and forms braced
- Add one extra bag per 10 as buffer

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Forgetting to convert inches to feet (4 in = 0.333 ft)
- Ordering the theoretical minimum — second trip costs more than 10% waste

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ
Q: What does the Concrete Calculator calculate?
A: The Concrete Calculator estimates concrete quantity for US jobs — Concrete volume in cubic yards and cubic feet, Ready-mix loads vs 40/60/80-lb bags, Weight and cost with waste. Enter dimensions, choose units, add waste and price to get instant results.

Q: How do I measure for concrete?
A: Measure length 20 ft x width 10 ft x depth 4 in on site. Use the unit selectors — the tool converts inches→feet (÷12) and yards→feet (×3) before math. For non-rectangular, split into rectangles and add.

Q: How much waste should I add?
A: Add 5–10% for spillage and uneven subgrade — 10% is safe for hand-screeded forms.

Q: How accurate is this concrete estimate?
A: Math is exact for your inputs. Actual needs vary by site, material and installation. Add 5–10% waste for rectangular, 10–15% for irregular.

Related: Concrete Volume Calculator (/concrete-volume-calculator/) | Concrete Cost Calculator (/concrete-cost-calculator/) | Concrete Pour Calculator (/concrete-pour-calculator/) | Concrete Mix Calculator (/concrete-mix-calculator/)

3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Volume = Length x Width x Depth
Variables:
  - L: Length in feet
  - W: Width in feet
  - D: Depth in feet

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in
Steps:
  - Convert depth: 4 in /12 = 0.333 ft
  - Cubic feet: 20 x 10 x 0.333 = 66.67 ft3
  - Cubic yards: 66.67 /27 = 2.47 yd3
  - With 10% waste: 2.72 yd3

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Concrete — 4000 psi typical for driveways, 3000 psi for patios. Ordered by cubic yard (27 ft³) or bags (0.60 ft³ per 80-lb). Keep slump 4 in for slabs.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Concrete — 4000 psi typical for driveways, 3000 psi for patios. Ordered by cubic yard (27 ft³) or bags (0.60 ft³ per 80-lb). Keep slump 4 in for slabs.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 5–10% for spillage and uneven subgrade — 10% is safe for hand-screeded forms.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Order ready-mix over ~1 yd³ (45 bags) — faster and cheaper
- Keep subgrade compacted and forms braced
- Add one extra bag per 10 as buffer

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Forgetting to convert inches to feet (4 in = 0.333 ft)
- Ordering the theoretical minimum — second trip costs more than 10% waste

[11] ASSUMPTIONS

Description: Free concrete stair calculator for US construction — estimate concrete stair quantity, material and cost with waste, US customary and metric units.

Canonical: https://calculatorst.com/concrete-stair-calculator/
Visual Variant: concrete
Breadcrumbs: Home > Construction > Concrete > Concrete Stair Calculator

[1] WHAT IS THIS CALCULATOR?
The Concrete Stair Calculator — Estimates concrete volume, weight and ready-mix versus bag count for US slabs, footings and walls.

[2] WHAT DOES IT CALCULATE?
- Concrete volume in cubic yards and cubic feet
- Ready-mix loads vs 40/60/80-lb bags
- Weight and cost with waste

[3] HOW TO USE
1. Measure Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in — use feet for length/width and inches for thickness where typical.
2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Volume = Length x Width x Depth
Variables:
  - L: Length in feet
  - W: Width in feet
  - D: Depth in feet

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in
Steps:
  - Convert depth: 4 in /12 = 0.333 ft
  - Cubic feet: 20 x 10 x 0.333 = 66.67 ft3
  - Cubic yards: 66.67 /27 = 2.47 yd3
  - With 10% waste: 2.72 yd3

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Concrete — 4000 psi typical for driveways, 3000 psi for patios. Ordered by cubic yard (27 ft³) or bags (0.60 ft³ per 80-lb). Keep slump 4 in for slabs.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Concrete — 4000 psi typical for driveways, 3000 psi for patios. Ordered by cubic yard (27 ft³) or bags (0.60 ft³ per 80-lb). Keep slump 4 in for slabs.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 5–10% for spillage and uneven subgrade — 10% is safe for hand-screeded forms.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Order ready-mix over ~1 yd³ (45 bags) — faster and cheaper
- Keep subgrade compacted and forms braced
- Add one extra bag per 10 as buffer

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Forgetting to convert inches to feet (4 in = 0.333 ft)
- Ordering the theoretical minimum — second trip costs more than 10% waste

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ

Category: Slab & Patio / Cluster: slab
Description: Free patio cost calculator for US construction — estimate patio cost quantity, material and cost with waste, US customary and metric units.
URL: https://calculatorst.com/patio-cost-calculator/
Canonical: https://calculatorst.com/patio-cost-calculator/
Visual Variant: slab
Breadcrumbs: Home > Construction > Slab & Patio > Patio Cost Calculator

[1] WHAT IS THIS CALCULATOR?
The Patio Cost Calculator — Sizes slabs and patio concrete for US patios and shed pads — area, thickness, reinforcement and cost.

[2] WHAT DOES IT CALCULATE?
- Slab area in square feet
- Concrete volume in cubic yards
- Bags and reinforcement with waste

[3] HOW TO USE
1. Measure Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in — use feet for length/width and inches for thickness where typical.
2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Volume = Length x Width x Depth
Variables:
  - L: Length in feet
  - W: Width in feet
  - D: Depth in feet

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in
Steps:
  - Convert depth: 4 in /12 = 0.333 ft
  - Cubic feet: 20 x 10 x 0.333 = 66.67 ft3
  - Cubic yards: 66.67 /27 = 2.47 yd3
  - With 10% waste: 2.72 yd3

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Patio slabs 3.5–4 in thick, 3000–3500 psi. Base 4 in compacted gravel. Use welded wire mesh or rebar for crack control.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Patio slabs 3.5–4 in thick, 3000–3500 psi. Base 4 in compacted gravel. Use welded wire mesh or rebar for crack control.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 5–10% for spillage and thickness variation — 10% safe for hand-screeded patios.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Slope patio 1/4 in per foot away from house
- Keep joints every 8–10 ft to control cracking

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Pouring too thin (under 3.5 in) — cracks and frost heave
- Forgetting slope — water pools against house

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ
Q: What does the Patio Cost Calculator calculate?
A: The Patio Cost Calculator estimates patio cost quantity for US jobs — Slab area in square feet, Concrete volume in cubic yards, Bags and reinforcement with waste. Enter dimensions, choose units, add waste and price to get instant results.

Q: How do I measure for patio cost?
A: Measure length 20 ft x width 10 ft x depth 4 in on site. Use the unit selectors — the tool converts inches→feet (÷12) and yards→feet (×3) before math. For non-rectangular, split into rectangles and add.

Q: How much waste should I add?
A: Add 5–10% for spillage and thickness variation — 10% safe for hand-screeded patios.

Q: How accurate is this patio cost estimate?
A: Math is exact for your inputs. Actual needs vary by site, material and installation. Add 5–10% waste for rectangular, 10–15% for irregular.

A: Measure length 20 ft x width 10 ft x depth 4 in on site. Use the unit selectors — the tool converts inches→feet (÷12) and yards→feet (×3) before math. For non-rectangular, split into rectangles and add.

Q: How much waste should I add?
A: Add 10% for over-excavation and slough — trench sides cave.

Q: How accurate is this basement wall estimate?
A: Math is exact for your inputs. Actual needs vary by site, material and installation. Add 5–10% waste for rectangular, 10–15% for irregular.

# Crawl Space Calculator — https://calculatorst.com/crawl-space-calculator/
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Slug: crawl-space-calculator
Title: Crawl Space Calculator — Material, Quantity & Cost
H1: Crawl Space Calculator
Category: Foundation / Cluster: foundation
Description: Free crawl space calculator for US construction — estimate crawl space quantity, material and cost with waste, US customary and metric units.
URL: https://calculatorst.com/crawl-space-calculator/
Canonical: https://calculatorst.com/crawl-space-calculator/
Visual Variant: foundation
Breadcrumbs: Home > Construction > Foundation > Crawl Space Calculator

[1] WHAT IS THIS CALCULATOR?
The Crawl Space Calculator — Estimates foundation excavation, footings and walls for US homes — volume, concrete and cost.

[2] WHAT DOES IT CALCULATE?
- Footing volume in cubic yards
- Foundation wall area and concrete
- Excavation volume with waste

[3] HOW TO USE
1. Measure Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in — use feet for length/width and inches for thickness where typical.
2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Volume = Length x Width x Depth
Variables:
  - L: Length in feet
  - W: Width in feet
  - D: Depth in feet

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in
Steps:
  - Convert depth: 4 in /12 = 0.333 ft
  - Cubic feet: 20 x 10 x 0.333 = 66.67 ft3
  - Cubic yards: 66.67 /27 = 2.47 yd3
  - With 10% waste: 2.72 yd3

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Footings 8–12 in thick, walls 8 in. Concrete 3000–4000 psi. Footing below frost line per local code.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Footings 8–12 in thick, walls 8 in. Concrete 3000–4000 psi. Footing below frost line per local code.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 10% for over-excavation and slough — trench sides cave.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Call 811 before digging
- Keep footings on undisturbed soil or compacted fill

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Shallow footings above frost line — heave
- Not adding extra for footing flares

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ

# Rebar Calculator — https://calculatorst.com/rebar-calculator/
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Slug: rebar-calculator
Title: Rebar Calculator — Quantity, Weight & Cost
H1: Rebar Calculator
Category: Rebar / Cluster: rebar
Description: Free rebar calculator for US construction — estimate rebar quantity, material and cost with waste, US customary and metric units.
URL: https://calculatorst.com/rebar-calculator/
Canonical: https://calculatorst.com/rebar-calculator/
Visual Variant: rebar
Breadcrumbs: Home > Construction > Rebar > Rebar Calculator

[1] WHAT IS THIS CALCULATOR?
The Rebar Calculator — Sizes rebar for US concrete — count, length, weight, spacing and cost with laps and chairs.

[2] WHAT DOES IT CALCULATE?
- Rebar count from spacing
- Total length and weight (lb/ft)
- Laps and chairs with waste

[3] HOW TO USE
1. Measure Wall 20 ft, spacing 16 in — use feet for length/width and inches for thickness where typical.
2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Rebar count = ceil(Length / Spacing) + 1
Variables:
  - Length: Total length in feet
  - Spacing: On-center spacing in inches

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Wall 20 ft, spacing 16 in
Steps:
  - Convert spacing: 16 in = 1.333 ft
  - Count: ceil(20 /1.333)+1 = 16
  - With 10% waste: 18

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Rebar #3 (0.376 lb/ft), #4 (0.668), #5 (1.043). Epoxy-coated for corrosive soils. Laps 40x diameter per ACI 318.Rebar laps 40x diameter per ACI 318. Chairs every 4 ft hold cover.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Rebar #3 (0.376 lb/ft), #4 (0.668), #5 (1.043). Epoxy-coated for corrosive soils. Laps 40x diameter per ACI 318.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 5% for cuts and laps — 10% for complex grids. Chairs every 4 ft.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Maintain 3 in cover to soil, 1.5 in to forms
- Tie with wire, not weld, unless specified

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Wrong lap length — 20x vs 40x diameter
- Too few chairs — rebar sinks to bottom

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ
Q: What does the Rebar Calculator calculate?
A: The Rebar Calculator estimates rebar quantity for US jobs — Rebar count from spacing, Total length and weight (lb/ft), Laps and chairs with waste. Enter dimensions, choose units, add waste and price to get instant results.

Q: How do I measure for rebar?
A: Measure wall 20 ft, spacing 16 in on site. Use the unit selectors — the tool converts inches→feet (÷12) and yards→feet (×3) before math. For non-rectangular, split into rectangles and add.

Q: How much waste should I add?
A: Add 5% for cuts and laps — 10% for complex grids. Chairs every 4 ft.

Q: How accurate is this rebar estimate?
A: Math is exact for your inputs. Actual needs vary by site, material and installation. For structural loads, have a qualified professional review your plans per code. Add 5–10% waste for rectangular, 10–15% for irregular.

Rebar count = ceil(Length / Spacing) + 1
Variables:
  - Length: Total length in feet
  - Spacing: On-center spacing in inches

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Wall 20 ft, spacing 16 in
Steps:
  - Convert spacing: 16 in = 1.333 ft
  - Count: ceil(20 /1.333)+1 = 16
  - With 10% waste: 18

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Rebar #3 (0.376 lb/ft), #4 (0.668), #5 (1.043). Epoxy-coated for corrosive soils. Laps 40x diameter per ACI 318.Rebar laps 40x diameter per ACI 318. Chairs every 4 ft hold cover.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Rebar #3 (0.376 lb/ft), #4 (0.668), #5 (1.043). Epoxy-coated for corrosive soils. Laps 40x diameter per ACI 318.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 5% for cuts and laps — 10% for complex grids. Chairs every 4 ft.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Maintain 3 in cover to soil, 1.5 in to forms
- Tie with wire, not weld, unless specified

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Wrong lap length — 20x vs 40x diameter
- Too few chairs — rebar sinks to bottom

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ

3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Volume = Length x Width x Depth
Variables:
  - L: Length in feet
  - W: Width in feet
  - D: Depth in feet

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in
Steps:
  - Convert depth: 4 in /12 = 0.333 ft
  - Cubic feet: 20 x 10 x 0.333 = 66.67 ft3
  - Cubic yards: 66.67 /27 = 2.47 yd3
  - With 10% waste: 2.72 yd3

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Rebar #3 (0.376 lb/ft), #4 (0.668), #5 (1.043). Epoxy-coated for corrosive soils. Laps 40x diameter per ACI 318.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

# Brick Calculator — https://calculatorst.com/brick-calculator/
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Slug: brick-calculator
Title: Brick Calculator — Quantity, Mortar & Cost
H1: Brick Calculator
Category: Brick & Masonry / Cluster: brick
Description: Free brick calculator for US construction — estimate brick quantity, material and cost with waste, US customary and metric units.
URL: https://calculatorst.com/brick-calculator/
Canonical: https://calculatorst.com/brick-calculator/
Visual Variant: brick
Breadcrumbs: Home > Construction > Brick & Masonry > Brick Calculator

[1] WHAT IS THIS CALCULATOR?
The Brick Calculator — Counts bricks and masonry units for US walls, patios and veneers — quantity, mortar and cost.

[2] WHAT DOES IT CALCULATE?
- Bricks or blocks per wall area
- Mortar volume with 3/8 in joint
- Waste-adjusted count and cost

[3] HOW TO USE
1. Measure Wall 20 ft x 8 ft, brick 7.5x2.25 in, joint 0.375 in — use feet for length/width and inches for thickness where typical.
2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Bricks = Wall area / Brick area (including mortar joint)
Variables:
  - Wall area: Length x Height in ft2
  - Brick area: (Brick L + joint) x (Brick H + joint)

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Wall 20 ft x 8 ft, brick 7.5x2.25 in, joint 0.375 in
Steps:
  - Wall area: 160 ft2 = 23,040 in2
  - Brick + joint: 7.875x2.625 = 20.67 in2
  - Bricks: ceil(23,040/20.67)=1,115
  - With 7% waste: 1,193

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Standard brick 7.5x2.25 in, 6.8 per ft2 with 3/8 in joint. Mortar Type S 1:3, Type N 1:4. CMU 16x8 in face (1.125 per ft2).Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Standard brick 7.5x2.25 in, 6.8 per ft2 with 3/8 in joint. Mortar Type S 1:3, Type N 1:4. CMU 16x8 in face (1.125 per ft2).

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 7% for cuts and breakage — 10% for arches or curves.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Dry-lay a course to check bond
- Keep mortar workable — do not retemper after initial set

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Forgetting joint thickness — count off 15%
- Not adding waste for cuts around openings

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ
Q: What does the Brick Calculator calculate?
A: The Brick Calculator estimates brick quantity for US jobs — Bricks or blocks per wall area, Mortar volume with 3/8 in joint, Waste-adjusted count and cost. Enter dimensions, choose units, add waste and price to get instant results.

Q: How do I measure for brick?
A: Measure wall 20 ft x 8 ft, brick 7.5x2.25 in, joint 0.375 in on site. Use the unit selectors — the tool converts inches→feet (÷12) and yards→feet (×3) before math. For non-rectangular, split into rectangles and add.

Q: How much waste should I add?
A: Add 7% for cuts and breakage — 10% for arches or curves.

Q: How accurate is this brick estimate?
A: Math is exact for your inputs. Actual needs vary by site, material and installation. Add 5–10% waste for rectangular, 10–15% for irregular.

Material: Standard brick 7.5x2.25 in, 6.8 per ft2 with 3/8 in joint. Mortar Type S 1:3, Type N 1:4. CMU 16x8 in face (1.125 per ft2).Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Standard brick 7.5x2.25 in, 6.8 per ft2 with 3/8 in joint. Mortar Type S 1:3, Type N 1:4. CMU 16x8 in face (1.125 per ft2).

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 7% for cuts and breakage — 10% for arches or curves.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Dry-lay a course to check bond
- Keep mortar workable — do not retemper after initial set

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Forgetting joint thickness — count off 15%
- Not adding waste for cuts around openings

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ
Q: What does the Brick Cost Calculator calculate?

Q: How do I measure for brick cost?
A: Measure wall 20 ft x 8 ft, brick 7.5x2.25 in, joint 0.375 in on site. Use the unit selectors — the tool converts inches→feet (÷12) and yards→feet (×3) before math. For non-rectangular, split into rectangles and add.

Q: How much waste should I add?
A: Add 7% for cuts and breakage — 10% for arches or curves.

Q: How accurate is this brick cost estimate?
A: Math is exact for your inputs. Actual needs vary by site, material and installation. Add 5–10% waste for rectangular, 10–15% for irregular.

Slug: brick-mortar-calculator
Title: Brick Mortar Calculator — Material, Quantity & Cost
H1: Brick Mortar Calculator
Category: Brick & Masonry / Cluster: brick
Description: Free brick mortar calculator for US construction — estimate brick mortar quantity, material and cost with waste, US customary and metric units.
URL: https://calculatorst.com/brick-mortar-calculator/
Canonical: https://calculatorst.com/brick-mortar-calculator/
Visual Variant: brick
Breadcrumbs: Home > Construction > Brick & Masonry > Brick Mortar Calculator

[1] WHAT IS THIS CALCULATOR?
The Brick Mortar Calculator — Counts bricks and masonry units for US walls, patios and veneers — quantity, mortar and cost.

[2] WHAT DOES IT CALCULATE?
- Bricks or blocks per wall area
- Mortar volume with 3/8 in joint
- Waste-adjusted count and cost

[3] HOW TO USE
1. Measure Wall 20 ft x 8 ft, brick 7.5x2.25 in, joint 0.375 in — use feet for length/width and inches for thickness where typical.
2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Bricks = Wall area / Brick area (including mortar joint)
Variables:
  - Wall area: Length x Height in ft2
  - Brick area: (Brick L + joint) x (Brick H + joint)

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Wall 20 ft x 8 ft, brick 7.5x2.25 in, joint 0.375 in
Steps:
  - Wall area: 160 ft2 = 23,040 in2
  - Brick + joint: 7.875x2.625 = 20.67 in2
  - Bricks: ceil(23,040/20.67)=1,115
  - With 7% waste: 1,193

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Standard brick 7.5x2.25 in, 6.8 per ft2 with 3/8 in joint. Mortar Type S 1:3, Type N 1:4. CMU 16x8 in face (1.125 per ft2).Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Standard brick 7.5x2.25 in, 6.8 per ft2 with 3/8 in joint. Mortar Type S 1:3, Type N 1:4. CMU 16x8 in face (1.125 per ft2).

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 7% for cuts and breakage — 10% for arches or curves.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Dry-lay a course to check bond
- Keep mortar workable — do not retemper after initial set

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Forgetting joint thickness — count off 15%
- Not adding waste for cuts around openings

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ

2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Bricks = Wall area / Brick area (including mortar joint)
Variables:
  - Wall area: Length x Height in ft2
  - Brick area: (Brick L + joint) x (Brick H + joint)

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Wall 20 ft x 8 ft, brick 7.5x2.25 in, joint 0.375 in
Steps:
  - Wall area: 160 ft2 = 23,040 in2
  - Brick + joint: 7.875x2.625 = 20.67 in2
  - Bricks: ceil(23,040/20.67)=1,115
  - With 7% waste: 1,193

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Standard brick 7.5x2.25 in, 6.8 per ft2 with 3/8 in joint. Mortar Type S 1:3, Type N 1:4. CMU 16x8 in face (1.125 per ft2).Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Standard brick 7.5x2.25 in, 6.8 per ft2 with 3/8 in joint. Mortar Type S 1:3, Type N 1:4. CMU 16x8 in face (1.125 per ft2).

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 7% for cuts and breakage — 10% for arches or curves.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Dry-lay a course to check bond
- Keep mortar workable — do not retemper after initial set

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Forgetting joint thickness — count off 15%
- Not adding waste for cuts around openings

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[4] FORMULA

Variables:
  - L: Length in feet
  - W: Width in feet
  - D: Depth in feet

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in
Steps:
  - Convert depth: 4 in /12 = 0.333 ft
  - Cubic feet: 20 x 10 x 0.333 = 66.67 ft3
  - Cubic yards: 66.67 /27 = 2.47 yd3
  - With 10% waste: 2.72 yd3

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: CMU 16x8 in face, 8 in thick. Grout 0.5 ft3 per core. Mortar 0.02 yd3 per 100 blocks.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

- Use half-blocks at openings to avoid cuts

2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Volume = Length x Width x Depth
Variables:
  - L: Length in feet
  - W: Width in feet
  - D: Depth in feet

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in
Steps:
  - Convert depth: 4 in /12 = 0.333 ft
  - Cubic feet: 20 x 10 x 0.333 = 66.67 ft3
  - Cubic yards: 66.67 /27 = 2.47 yd3
  - With 10% waste: 2.72 yd3

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: CMU 16x8 in face, 8 in thick. Grout 0.5 ft3 per core. Mortar 0.02 yd3 per 100 blocks.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
CMU 16x8 in face, 8 in thick. Grout 0.5 ft3 per core. Mortar 0.02 yd3 per 100 blocks.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 5% for cuts and breakage — 7% for pilasters.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Stagger joints half-block
- Use half-blocks at openings to avoid cuts

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Not deducting openings — over-order 10%
- Forgetting grout for reinforced cells

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

# Concrete Block Wall Calculator — https://calculatorst.com/concrete-block-wall-calculator/
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Slug: concrete-block-wall-calculator
Title: Concrete Block Wall Calculator — Material, Quantity & Cost
H1: Concrete Block Wall Calculator
Category: Concrete Block / Cluster: cmu
Description: Free concrete block wall calculator for US construction — estimate concrete block wall quantity, material and cost with waste, US customary and metric units.
URL: https://calculatorst.com/concrete-block-wall-calculator/
Canonical: https://calculatorst.com/concrete-block-wall-calculator/
Visual Variant: cmu
Breadcrumbs: Home > Construction > Concrete Block > Concrete Block Wall Calculator

[1] WHAT IS THIS CALCULATOR?
The Concrete Block Wall Calculator — Counts CMU and concrete blocks for US walls — units, grout, mortar and reinforcement.

[2] WHAT DOES IT CALCULATE?
- CMU count per wall area
- Grout and mortar volume
- Reinforcement with waste

[3] HOW TO USE
1. Measure Wall 20 ft x 8 ft, brick 7.5x2.25 in, joint 0.375 in — use feet for length/width and inches for thickness where typical.
2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Bricks = Wall area / Brick area (including mortar joint)
Variables:
  - Wall area: Length x Height in ft2
  - Brick area: (Brick L + joint) x (Brick H + joint)

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Wall 20 ft x 8 ft, brick 7.5x2.25 in, joint 0.375 in
Steps:
  - Wall area: 160 ft2 = 23,040 in2
  - Brick + joint: 7.875x2.625 = 20.67 in2
  - Bricks: ceil(23,040/20.67)=1,115
  - With 7% waste: 1,193

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: CMU 16x8 in face, 8 in thick. Grout 0.5 ft3 per core. Mortar 0.02 yd3 per 100 blocks.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
CMU 16x8 in face, 8 in thick. Grout 0.5 ft3 per core. Mortar 0.02 yd3 per 100 blocks.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 5% for cuts and breakage — 7% for pilasters.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Stagger joints half-block
- Use half-blocks at openings to avoid cuts

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Not deducting openings — over-order 10%
- Forgetting grout for reinforced cells

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ

CMU 16x8 in face, 8 in thick. Grout 0.5 ft3 per core. Mortar 0.02 yd3 per 100 blocks.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 5% for cuts and breakage — 7% for pilasters.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Stagger joints half-block
- Use half-blocks at openings to avoid cuts

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Not deducting openings — over-order 10%
- Forgetting grout for reinforced cells

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[1] WHAT IS THIS CALCULATOR?
The Mortar Calculator — Mixes mortar, grout and cement for US masonry — volume, bags and sand ratios.

[2] WHAT DOES IT CALCULATE?
- Mortar volume in cubic feet
- Cement bags (94-lb) and sand
- Water and cost with waste

[3] HOW TO USE
1. Measure Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in — use feet for length/width and inches for thickness where typical.
2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Volume = Length x Width x Depth
Variables:
  - L: Length in feet
  - W: Width in feet
  - D: Depth in feet

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in
Steps:
  - Convert depth: 4 in /12 = 0.333 ft
  - Cubic feet: 20 x 10 x 0.333 = 66.67 ft3
  - Cubic yards: 66.67 /27 = 2.47 yd3
  - With 10% waste: 2.72 yd3

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Portland cement 94-lb bag, 1 ft3. Mortar Type S 1:3, Type M 1:2, Type N 1:4. Grout 1:2 cement:sand.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Portland cement 94-lb bag, 1 ft3. Mortar Type S 1:3, Type M 1:2, Type N 1:4. Grout 1:2 cement:sand.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 10% for spillage and retemper — mortar stiffens in 90 min.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Mix small batches — do not add water after stiffening
- Keep sand damp for consistent mix

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Wrong ratio — weak mortar cracks
- Too much water — weak and shrinks

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

A: Math is exact for your inputs. Actual needs vary by site, material and installation. Add 5–10% waste for rectangular, 10–15% for irregular.

A: Math is exact for your inputs. Actual needs vary by site, material and installation. Add 5–10% waste for rectangular, 10–15% for irregular.

Title: Trench Volume Calculator — Material, Quantity & Cost
H1: Trench Volume Calculator
Category: Excavation / Cluster: excavation
Description: Free trench volume calculator for US construction — estimate trench volume quantity, material and cost with waste, US customary and metric units.

Canonical: https://calculatorst.com/trench-volume-calculator/
Visual Variant: excavation
Breadcrumbs: Home > Construction > Excavation > Trench Volume Calculator

[1] WHAT IS THIS CALCULATOR?
The Trench Volume Calculator — Volumes earthwork for US trenches and grading — cut, fill, backfill and soil weight.

[2] WHAT DOES IT CALCULATE?
- Trench volume in cubic yards
- Backfill less pipe volume
- Soil weight in tons with swell

[3] HOW TO USE
1. Measure Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in — use feet for length/width and inches for thickness where typical.
2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Volume = Length x Width x Depth
Variables:
  - L: Length in feet
  - W: Width in feet
  - D: Depth in feet

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in
Steps:
  - Convert depth: 4 in /12 = 0.333 ft
  - Cubic feet: 20 x 10 x 0.333 = 66.67 ft3
  - Cubic yards: 66.67 /27 = 2.47 yd3
  - With 10% waste: 2.72 yd3

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Soil swells 15–25% when dug (1.2x). Clay 1.6 t/yd3, sand 1.5, topsoil 1.2. Call 811 before dig.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Soil swells 15–25% when dug (1.2x). Clay 1.6 t/yd3, sand 1.5, topsoil 1.2. Call 811 before dig.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Trench sides slough — add 10% for over-excavation. Shrinkage 10% on backfill.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Bench or slope sides per OSHA over 5 ft
- Stockpile topsoil separate from subsoil

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Forgetting swell — truck count short
- Not subtracting pipe volume for backfill

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ
Q: What does the Trench Volume Calculator calculate?

Q: How do I measure for trench volume?
A: Measure length 20 ft x width 10 ft x depth 4 in on site. Use the unit selectors — the tool converts inches→feet (÷12) and yards→feet (×3) before math. For non-rectangular, split into rectangles and add.

Q: How much waste should I add?
A: Trench sides slough — add 10% for over-excavation. Shrinkage 10% on backfill.

Breadcrumbs: Home > Construction > Framing & Lumber > Framing Calculator

[1] WHAT IS THIS CALCULATOR?
The Framing Calculator — Frames US walls and floors — studs, joists, headers, beams and board feet.

[2] WHAT DOES IT CALCULATE?
- Stud count from spacing
- Board feet (T x W x L /12)
- Joist/beam loads with waste

[3] HOW TO USE
1. Measure Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in — use feet for length/width and inches for thickness where typical.
2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Volume = Length x Width x Depth
Variables:
  - L: Length in feet
  - W: Width in feet
  - D: Depth in feet

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in
Steps:
  - Convert depth: 4 in /12 = 0.333 ft
  - Cubic feet: 20 x 10 x 0.333 = 66.67 ft3
  - Cubic yards: 66.67 /27 = 2.47 yd3
  - With 10% waste: 2.72 yd3

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Studs 2x4/2x6, 16 in o.c. typical. SPF #2, 1.5x3.5 in actual. Board foot = 1x12x12 in.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Studs 2x4/2x6, 16 in o.c. typical. SPF #2, 1.5x3.5 in actual. Board foot = 1x12x12 in.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 10% for cuts and cripples — 15% for gable walls.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Add 1 stud per corner and opening
- Crown joists up

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- 16 in vs 24 in o.c. — count off 30%
- Forgetting cripples under sills

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ
Q: What does the Framing Calculator calculate?
A: The Framing Calculator estimates framing quantity for US jobs — Stud count from spacing, Board feet (T x W x L /12), Joist/beam loads with waste. Enter dimensions, choose units, add waste and price to get instant results.

Q: How do I measure for framing?
A: Measure length 20 ft x width 10 ft x depth 4 in on site. Use the unit selectors — the tool converts inches→feet (÷12) and yards→feet (×3) before math. For non-rectangular, split into rectangles and add.

Q: How much waste should I add?
A: Add 10% for cuts and cripples — 15% for gable walls.

Q: How accurate is this framing estimate?
A: Math is exact for your inputs. Actual needs vary by site, material and installation. Add 5–10% waste for rectangular, 10–15% for irregular.

Studs 2x4/2x6, 16 in o.c. typical. SPF #2, 1.5x3.5 in actual. Board foot = 1x12x12 in.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 10% for cuts and cripples — 15% for gable walls.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Add 1 stud per corner and opening
- Crown joists up

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- 16 in vs 24 in o.c. — count off 30%
- Forgetting cripples under sills

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ

Q: How do I measure for board foot cost?
A: Measure length 20 ft x width 10 ft x depth 4 in on site. Use the unit selectors — the tool converts inches→feet (÷12) and yards→feet (×3) before math. For non-rectangular, split into rectangles and add.

Q: How much waste should I add?
A: Add 10% for cuts and cripples — 15% for gable walls.

Q: How accurate is this board foot cost estimate?
A: Math is exact for your inputs. Actual needs vary by site, material and installation. Add 5–10% waste for rectangular, 10–15% for irregular.

A: Measure length 20 ft x width 10 ft x depth 4 in on site. Use the unit selectors — the tool converts inches→feet (÷12) and yards→feet (×3) before math. For non-rectangular, split into rectangles and add.

Q: How much waste should I add?
A: Add 10% for cuts and cripples — 15% for gable walls.

Q: How accurate is this beam load estimate?
A: Math is exact for your inputs. Actual needs vary by site, material and installation. For structural loads, have a qualified professional review your plans per code. Add 5–10% waste for rectangular, 10–15% for irregular.

Canonical: https://calculatorst.com/roofing-calculator/
Visual Variant: roofing
Breadcrumbs: Home > Construction > Roofing > Roofing Calculator

[1] WHAT IS THIS CALCULATOR?
The Roofing Calculator — Measures US roofs — area, pitch, shingles, underlayment and rafters with waste.

[2] WHAT DOES IT CALCULATE?
- Roof area via pitch multiplier
- Shingle bundles and squares (100 ft2)
- Underlayment and waste

[3] HOW TO USE
1. Measure Footprint 30x40 ft, pitch 6:12, overhang 1.5 ft — use feet for length/width and inches for thickness where typical.
2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Multiplier = sqrt(pitch^2+144) /12  •  Roof area = Footprint x Multiplier
Variables:
  - pitch: Rise per 12" run

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Footprint 30x40 ft, pitch 6:12, overhang 1.5 ft
Steps:
  - Footprint + overhang: 33x43 = 1,419 ft2
  - Multiplier: sqrt(36+144)/12 = 1.118
  - Roof area: 1,419x1.118 = 1,586 ft2
  - Squares: 15.86 sq

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Shingles 3 bundles per square, 33 ft2 each. Underlayment 2 squares per roll. Drip edge linear feet.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Shingles 3 bundles per square, 33 ft2 each. Underlayment 2 squares per roll. Drip edge linear feet.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 10% for gable, 15% for hip/valley — cuts add waste.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Order ridge caps separate
- Valley metal 10% extra

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Using footprint not roof area — short 12% at 6:12
- Forgetting starter strip

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ
Q: What does the Roofing Calculator calculate?

Description: Free roofing material calculator for US construction — estimate roofing material quantity, material and cost with waste, US customary and metric units.
URL: https://calculatorst.com/roofing-material-calculator/
Canonical: https://calculatorst.com/roofing-material-calculator/
Visual Variant: roofing
Breadcrumbs: Home > Construction > Roofing > Roofing Material Calculator

[1] WHAT IS THIS CALCULATOR?
The Roofing Material Calculator — Measures US roofs — area, pitch, shingles, underlayment and rafters with waste.

[2] WHAT DOES IT CALCULATE?
- Roof area via pitch multiplier
- Shingle bundles and squares (100 ft2)
- Underlayment and waste

[3] HOW TO USE
1. Measure Footprint 30x40 ft, pitch 6:12, overhang 1.5 ft — use feet for length/width and inches for thickness where typical.
2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Multiplier = sqrt(pitch^2+144) /12  •  Roof area = Footprint x Multiplier
Variables:
  - pitch: Rise per 12" run

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Footprint 30x40 ft, pitch 6:12, overhang 1.5 ft
Steps:
  - Footprint + overhang: 33x43 = 1,419 ft2
  - Multiplier: sqrt(36+144)/12 = 1.118
  - Roof area: 1,419x1.118 = 1,586 ft2
  - Squares: 15.86 sq

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Shingles 3 bundles per square, 33 ft2 each. Underlayment 2 squares per roll. Drip edge linear feet.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Shingles 3 bundles per square, 33 ft2 each. Underlayment 2 squares per roll. Drip edge linear feet.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 10% for gable, 15% for hip/valley — cuts add waste.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Order ridge caps separate
- Valley metal 10% extra

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Using footprint not roof area — short 12% at 6:12
- Forgetting starter strip

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ
Q: What does the Roofing Material Calculator calculate?
A: The Roofing Material Calculator estimates roofing material quantity for US jobs — Roof area via pitch multiplier, Shingle bundles and squares (100 ft2), Underlayment and waste. Enter dimensions, choose units, add waste and price to get instant results.

Q: How do I measure for roofing material?
A: Measure footprint 30x40 ft, pitch 6:12, overhang 1.5 ft on site. Use the unit selectors — the tool converts inches→feet (÷12) and yards→feet (×3) before math. For non-rectangular, split into rectangles and add.

Q: How much waste should I add?
A: Add 10% for gable, 15% for hip/valley — cuts add waste.

Q: How accurate is this roofing material estimate?
A: Math is exact for your inputs. Actual needs vary by site, material and installation. Add 5–10% waste for rectangular, 10–15% for irregular.

Multiplier = sqrt(pitch^2+144) /12  •  Roof area = Footprint x Multiplier
Variables:
  - pitch: Rise per 12" run

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Footprint 30x40 ft, pitch 6:12, overhang 1.5 ft
Steps:
  - Footprint + overhang: 33x43 = 1,419 ft2
  - Multiplier: sqrt(36+144)/12 = 1.118
  - Roof area: 1,419x1.118 = 1,586 ft2
  - Squares: 15.86 sq

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Shingles 3 bundles per square, 33 ft2 each. Underlayment 2 squares per roll. Drip edge linear feet.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Shingles 3 bundles per square, 33 ft2 each. Underlayment 2 squares per roll. Drip edge linear feet.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 10% for gable, 15% for hip/valley — cuts add waste.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Order ridge caps separate
- Valley metal 10% extra

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Using footprint not roof area — short 12% at 6:12
- Forgetting starter strip

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ
Q: What does the Roof Waste Calculator calculate?
A: The Roof Waste Calculator estimates roof waste quantity for US jobs — Roof area via pitch multiplier, Shingle bundles and squares (100 ft2), Underlayment and waste. Enter dimensions, choose units, add waste and price to get instant results.

Q: How do I measure for roof waste?
A: Measure footprint 30x40 ft, pitch 6:12, overhang 1.5 ft on site. Use the unit selectors — the tool converts inches→feet (÷12) and yards→feet (×3) before math. For non-rectangular, split into rectangles and add.

Q: How much waste should I add?
A: Add 10% for gable, 15% for hip/valley — cuts add waste.

Q: How accurate is this roof waste estimate?
A: Math is exact for your inputs. Actual needs vary by site, material and installation. Add 5–10% waste for rectangular, 10–15% for irregular.

# Underlayment Calculator — https://calculatorst.com/underlayment-calculator/
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Slug: underlayment-calculator
Title: Underlayment Calculator — Material, Quantity & Cost
H1: Underlayment Calculator
Category: Flooring & Tile / Cluster: flooring
Description: Free underlayment calculator for US construction — estimate underlayment quantity, material and cost with waste, US customary and metric units.
URL: https://calculatorst.com/underlayment-calculator/
Canonical: https://calculatorst.com/underlayment-calculator/
Visual Variant: flooring
Breadcrumbs: Home > Construction > Flooring & Tile > Underlayment Calculator

[1] WHAT IS THIS CALCULATOR?
The Underlayment Calculator — Covers US floors — hardwood, laminate, vinyl, carpet and tile with grout and waste.

[2] WHAT DOES IT CALCULATE?
- Floor area in square feet
- Boxes or rolls with waste
- Grout and adhesive

[3] HOW TO USE
1. Measure Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in — use feet for length/width and inches for thickness where typical.
2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Volume = Length x Width x Depth
Variables:
  - L: Length in feet
  - W: Width in feet
  - D: Depth in feet

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in
Steps:
  - Convert depth: 4 in /12 = 0.333 ft
  - Cubic feet: 20 x 10 x 0.333 = 66.67 ft3
  - Cubic yards: 66.67 /27 = 2.47 yd3
  - With 10% waste: 2.72 yd3

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Tile 12x12 in = 1 ft2, 6.8 per ft2 with grout. Hardwood 20 ft2 per box. Carpet 12 ft wide rolls.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Tile 12x12 in = 1 ft2, 6.8 per ft2 with grout. Hardwood 20 ft2 per box. Carpet 12 ft wide rolls.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 10% for straight, 15% for diagonal or large tile. Pattern adds 20%.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Acclimate wood 72 hours
- Stagger joints 6 in

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Not adding waste for diagonal — short 15%
- Forgetting grout joint — count off

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ
Q: What does the Underlayment Calculator calculate?
A: The Underlayment Calculator estimates underlayment quantity for US jobs — Floor area in square feet, Boxes or rolls with waste, Grout and adhesive. Enter dimensions, choose units, add waste and price to get instant results.

Q: How do I measure for underlayment?
A: Measure length 20 ft x width 10 ft x depth 4 in on site. Use the unit selectors — the tool converts inches→feet (÷12) and yards→feet (×3) before math. For non-rectangular, split into rectangles and add.

Q: How much waste should I add?
A: Add 10% for straight, 15% for diagonal or large tile. Pattern adds 20%.

Q: How accurate is this underlayment estimate?
A: Math is exact for your inputs. Actual needs vary by site, material and installation. Add 5–10% waste for rectangular, 10–15% for irregular.

[1] WHAT IS THIS CALCULATOR?
The Drywall Calculator — Covers US interiors — drywall sheets, screws, tape, paint and insulation.

[2] WHAT DOES IT CALCULATE?
- Sheets (4x8 =32 ft2)
- Gallons at 350 ft2/gal
- Insulation batts with waste

[3] HOW TO USE
1. Measure Room 12x10 ft, height 8 ft — use feet for length/width and inches for thickness where typical.
2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Quantity = Area / Coverage per unit
Variables:
  - Area: Length x Width in ft2

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Room 12x10 ft, height 8 ft
Steps:
  - Wall area: 2x(12x8)+2x(10x8)=352 ft2
  - Less openings: -21 ft2 door =331 ft2
  - Gallons @350 ft2/gal: ceil(331/350)=1

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Drywall 4x8, screws 28 per sheet, tape 500 ft per roll. Paint 350 ft2/gal, primer 300. Spray foam 1 in = R6.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Drywall 4x8, screws 28 per sheet, tape 500 ft per roll. Paint 350 ft2/gal, primer 300. Spray foam 1 in = R6.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 10% for cuts and openings — 15% for vaulted ceilings.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Hang drywall perpendicular to joists
- Prime before paint for true color

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Not deducting openings — over-order 10%
- One coat paint — needs two for coverage

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

H1: Paint Coverage Calculator

Description: Free paint coverage calculator for US construction — estimate paint coverage quantity, material and cost with waste, US customary and metric units.

Canonical: https://calculatorst.com/paint-coverage-calculator/
Visual Variant: drywall
Breadcrumbs: Home > Construction > Drywall & Paint > Paint Coverage Calculator

[1] WHAT IS THIS CALCULATOR?
The Paint Coverage Calculator — Covers US interiors — drywall sheets, screws, tape, paint and insulation.

[2] WHAT DOES IT CALCULATE?
- Sheets (4x8 =32 ft2)
- Gallons at 350 ft2/gal
- Insulation batts with waste

[3] HOW TO USE
1. Measure Room 12x10 ft, height 8 ft — use feet for length/width and inches for thickness where typical.
2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Quantity = Area / Coverage per unit
Variables:
  - Area: Length x Width in ft2

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Room 12x10 ft, height 8 ft
Steps:
  - Wall area: 2x(12x8)+2x(10x8)=352 ft2
  - Less openings: -21 ft2 door =331 ft2
  - Gallons @350 ft2/gal: ceil(331/350)=1

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Drywall 4x8, screws 28 per sheet, tape 500 ft per roll. Paint 350 ft2/gal, primer 300. Spray foam 1 in = R6.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Drywall 4x8, screws 28 per sheet, tape 500 ft per roll. Paint 350 ft2/gal, primer 300. Spray foam 1 in = R6.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 10% for cuts and openings — 15% for vaulted ceilings.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Hang drywall perpendicular to joists
- Prime before paint for true color

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Not deducting openings — over-order 10%
- One coat paint — needs two for coverage

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ
Q: What does the Paint Coverage Calculator calculate?
A: The Paint Coverage Calculator estimates paint coverage quantity for US jobs — Sheets (4x8 =32 ft2), Gallons at 350 ft2/gal, Insulation batts with waste. Enter dimensions, choose units, add waste and price to get instant results.

Q: How do I measure for paint coverage?
A: Measure room 12x10 ft, height 8 ft on site. Use the unit selectors — the tool converts inches→feet (÷12) and yards→feet (×3) before math. For non-rectangular, split into rectangles and add.

Q: How much waste should I add?
A: Add 10% for cuts and openings — 15% for vaulted ceilings.

Q: How accurate is this paint coverage estimate?
A: Math is exact for your inputs. Actual needs vary by site, material and installation. Add 5–10% waste for rectangular, 10–15% for irregular.

# Primer Calculator — https://calculatorst.com/primer-calculator/
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Slug: primer-calculator
Title: Primer Calculator — Material, Quantity & Cost
H1: Primer Calculator
Category: Drywall & Paint / Cluster: drywall
Description: Free primer calculator for US construction — estimate primer quantity, material and cost with waste, US customary and metric units.
URL: https://calculatorst.com/primer-calculator/
Canonical: https://calculatorst.com/primer-calculator/
Visual Variant: drywall
Breadcrumbs: Home > Construction > Drywall & Paint > Primer Calculator

[1] WHAT IS THIS CALCULATOR?
The Primer Calculator — Covers US interiors — drywall sheets, screws, tape, paint and insulation.

[2] WHAT DOES IT CALCULATE?
- Sheets (4x8 =32 ft2)
- Gallons at 350 ft2/gal
- Insulation batts with waste

[3] HOW TO USE
1. Measure Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in — use feet for length/width and inches for thickness where typical.
2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Volume = Length x Width x Depth
Variables:
  - L: Length in feet
  - W: Width in feet
  - D: Depth in feet

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in
Steps:
  - Convert depth: 4 in /12 = 0.333 ft
  - Cubic feet: 20 x 10 x 0.333 = 66.67 ft3
  - Cubic yards: 66.67 /27 = 2.47 yd3
  - With 10% waste: 2.72 yd3

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Drywall 4x8, screws 28 per sheet, tape 500 ft per roll. Paint 350 ft2/gal, primer 300. Spray foam 1 in = R6.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Drywall 4x8, screws 28 per sheet, tape 500 ft per roll. Paint 350 ft2/gal, primer 300. Spray foam 1 in = R6.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 10% for cuts and openings — 15% for vaulted ceilings.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Hang drywall perpendicular to joists
- Prime before paint for true color

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Not deducting openings — over-order 10%
- One coat paint — needs two for coverage

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ
Q: What does the Primer Calculator calculate?
A: The Primer Calculator estimates primer quantity for US jobs — Sheets (4x8 =32 ft2), Gallons at 350 ft2/gal, Insulation batts with waste. Enter dimensions, choose units, add waste and price to get instant results.

Q: How do I measure for primer?
A: Measure length 20 ft x width 10 ft x depth 4 in on site. Use the unit selectors — the tool converts inches→feet (÷12) and yards→feet (×3) before math. For non-rectangular, split into rectangles and add.

Q: How much waste should I add?
A: Add 10% for cuts and openings — 15% for vaulted ceilings.

Q: How accurate is this primer estimate?
A: Math is exact for your inputs. Actual needs vary by site, material and installation. Add 5–10% waste for rectangular, 10–15% for irregular.

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Not deducting openings — over-order 10%
- One coat paint — needs two for coverage

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ
Q: What does the Spray Foam Calculator calculate?
A: The Spray Foam Calculator estimates spray foam quantity for US jobs — Sheets (4x8 =32 ft2), Gallons at 350 ft2/gal, Insulation batts with waste. Enter dimensions, choose units, add waste and price to get instant results.

Q: How do I measure for spray foam?
A: Measure length 20 ft x width 10 ft x depth 4 in on site. Use the unit selectors — the tool converts inches→feet (÷12) and yards→feet (×3) before math. For non-rectangular, split into rectangles and add.

Q: How much waste should I add?
A: Add 10% for cuts and openings — 15% for vaulted ceilings.

Q: How accurate is this spray foam estimate?
A: Math is exact for your inputs. Actual needs vary by site, material and installation. Add 5–10% waste for rectangular, 10–15% for irregular.

Title: Fence Calculator — Material, Quantity & Cost
H1: Fence Calculator
Category: Deck & Fence / Cluster: deck-fence
Description: Free fence calculator for US construction — estimate fence quantity, material and cost with waste, US customary and metric units.
URL: https://calculatorst.com/fence-calculator/
Canonical: https://calculatorst.com/fence-calculator/
Visual Variant: deck-fence
Breadcrumbs: Home > Construction > Deck & Fence > Fence Calculator

[1] WHAT IS THIS CALCULATOR?
The Fence Calculator — Builds US decks and fences — boards, joists, posts, panels and concrete with waste.

[2] WHAT DOES IT CALCULATE?
- Deck boards and joists
- Fence posts, panels and rails
- Concrete bags per post

[3] HOW TO USE
1. Measure Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in — use feet for length/width and inches for thickness where typical.
2. Enter values and select units. The calculator converts to feet before calculating (inches /12, yards x3).
3. Adjust waste % and optional price, then click Calculate. Results show with waste included.
4. Use Reset to clear and try another size.

[4] FORMULA
Volume = Length x Width x Depth
Variables:
  - L: Length in feet
  - W: Width in feet
  - D: Depth in feet

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in
Steps:
  - Convert depth: 4 in /12 = 0.333 ft
  - Cubic feet: 20 x 10 x 0.333 = 66.67 ft3
  - Cubic yards: 66.67 /27 = 2.47 yd3
  - With 10% waste: 2.72 yd3

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Deck boards 5.5 in + 1/8 in gap, joists 16 in o.c. Fence posts 8 ft spacing, 2.5 ft deep, 0.5 bag 50-lb per post.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Deck boards 5.5 in + 1/8 in gap, joists 16 in o.c. Fence posts 8 ft spacing, 2.5 ft deep, 0.5 bag 50-lb per post.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 10% for cuts and angles — 15% for curves.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Crown deck boards up
- Set posts in gravel + concrete for drainage

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Wrong gap — boards buckle
- Shallow posts — frost heave

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ
Q: What does the Fence Calculator calculate?
A: The Fence Calculator estimates fence quantity for US jobs — Deck boards and joists, Fence posts, panels and rails, Concrete bags per post. Enter dimensions, choose units, add waste and price to get instant results.

Q: How do I measure for fence?
A: Measure length 20 ft x width 10 ft x depth 4 in on site. Use the unit selectors — the tool converts inches→feet (÷12) and yards→feet (×3) before math. For non-rectangular, split into rectangles and add.

Q: How much waste should I add?
A: Add 10% for cuts and angles — 15% for curves.

Q: How accurate is this fence estimate?
A: Math is exact for your inputs. Actual needs vary by site, material and installation. Add 5–10% waste for rectangular, 10–15% for irregular.

Related: Deck Calculator (/deck-calculator/) | Deck Cost Calculator (/deck-cost-calculator/) | Deck Board Calculator (/deck-board-calculator/) | Deck Joist Calculator (/deck-joist-calculator/)

  - With 10% waste: 2.72 yd3

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Deck boards 5.5 in + 1/8 in gap, joists 16 in o.c. Fence posts 8 ft spacing, 2.5 ft deep, 0.5 bag 50-lb per post.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Deck boards 5.5 in + 1/8 in gap, joists 16 in o.c. Fence posts 8 ft spacing, 2.5 ft deep, 0.5 bag 50-lb per post.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 10% for cuts and angles — 15% for curves.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Crown deck boards up
- Set posts in gravel + concrete for drainage

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Wrong gap — boards buckle
- Shallow posts — frost heave

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

- Slope 2% for drainage

- Thin asphalt — cracks in year
- No base — base failure, not asphalt

Variables:
  - L: Length in feet
  - W: Width in feet
  - D: Depth in feet

[5] WORKED EXAMPLE
Inputs: Length 20 ft x Width 10 ft x Depth 4 in
Steps:
  - Convert depth: 4 in /12 = 0.333 ft
  - Cubic feet: 20 x 10 x 0.333 = 66.67 ft3
  - Cubic yards: 66.67 /27 = 2.47 yd3
  - With 10% waste: 2.72 yd3

[6] CONSTRUCTION INFORMATION
Material: Asphalt 145 lb/ft3 (2.025 t/yd3), 2–3 in driveways, 4 in commercial. Tack coat 0.05 gal/ft2.Measure longest x widest; split L-shapes into two rectangles and add.Compact subgrade — soft spots add thickness.For structural sizing (beams, headers, rebar) verify with a professional per local code.

[7] MATERIAL INFORMATION
Asphalt 145 lb/ft3 (2.025 t/yd3), 2–3 in driveways, 4 in commercial. Tack coat 0.05 gal/ft2.

[8] WASTE & OVERAGES
Add 5% for screed loss — 10% for hand work.

[9] PRACTICAL TIPS
- Compact while hot (>275F)
- Slope 2% for drainage

[10] COMMON MISTAKES
- Thin asphalt — cracks in year
- No base — base failure, not asphalt

[11] ASSUMPTIONS
This calculator provides an estimate based on your inputs. Actual requirements vary by site, material and installation. For code-regulated or load-bearing work, consult a qualified professional.

[12] FAQ
Q: What does the Asphalt Thickness Calculator calculate?
A: The Asphalt Thickness Calculator estimates asphalt thickness quantity for US jobs — Asphalt tons from volume x 2.025 t/yd3, Thickness and area, Parking spaces with waste. Enter dimensions, choose units, add waste and price to get instant results.

Q: How do I measure for asphalt thickness?
A: Measure length 20 ft x width 10 ft x depth 4 in on site. Use the unit selectors — the tool converts inches→feet (÷12) and yards→feet (×3) before math. For non-rectangular, split into rectangles and add.

Q: How much waste should I add?
A: Add 5% for screed loss — 10% for hand work.

Q: How accurate is this asphalt thickness estimate?
A: Math is exact for your inputs. Actual needs vary by site, material and installation. Add 5–10% waste for rectangular, 10–15% for irregular.

================================================================================
URL STRUCTURE & ROUTING
================================================================================
Pattern: /{slug}/ — lowercase, hyphen-separated, trailingSlash always (astro.config.mjs)
Examples: /concrete-calculator/, /concrete-slab-calculator/, /rebar-calculator/, /brick-calculator/, /gravel-calculator/
Avoid: /calculator?id=123
Sitemap: src/pages/sitemap.xml.ts — 229 URLs (200 calculators + 17 hubs + 12 static)
Robots: src/pages/robots.txt.ts — Allow:/ + Disallow:/*?*

================================================================================
REUSABLE COMPONENTS
================================================================================
- src/layouts/Layout.astro — SEOHead (title, desc, canonical, OG, JSON-LD WebSite/Organization/WebPage)
- src/components/CalculatorLayout.astro — slots: header, calculator, visual, info, related, FAQ, footer; injects SoftwareApplication + HowTo
- src/components/calculators/shared/CalculatorInput.astro — label + input + error
- src/components/calculators/shared/UnitSelector.astro — select with units
- src/components/calculators/shared/ResultCard.astro — hidden until Calculate
- src/components/calculators/shared/FormulaSection.astro — formula + variables
- src/components/calculators/shared/ExampleSection.astro — table steps
- src/components/calculators/shared/CategoryCard.astro — hub cards
- src/components/calculators/GenericCalculator.astro — id, inputs[], formulaKey (volume/area/quantity), waste, price, fmt, toFeet, validation
- src/components/CalculatorVisual.astro — variant switch (16 SVG + real photos + 3D model-viewer for 4 heroes)
- src/components/Calculator3D.astro — <model-viewer> wrapper, lazy, AR
- src/components/Breadcrumbs.astro — BreadcrumbList JSON-LD
- src/components/FAQ.astro — FAQPage JSON-LD, details/summary
- src/components/RelatedCalculators.astro — 4 within cluster
- src/utils/units.ts — toFeet, toInches
- src/utils/format.ts — fmt, fmtCurrency

================================================================================
VISUALS — PER CALCULATOR (ORIGINAL, NOT COPIED)
================================================================================
Each page has at least one useful visual: inline SVG technical diagram (top view + cross-section, measurement arrows, material texture) + Unsplash real photo 800w lazy (https: allowed in CSP) + 4 heroes 3D model-viewer (concrete, deck, roof-pitch, roof-area) lazy, AR, neutral env.
Examples:
- Concrete Slab: Length/Width/Thickness + isometric slab with rebar grid + thickness arrow 4"
- Roofing: Roof length/width/pitch/slope/area + pitch triangle rise/run/angle 26.6deg
- Fence: Fence length/post spacing/post locations/height + gate
- Gravel: Length/Width/Depth + cross-section depth
- Brick: Wall length/height/brick dimensions/mortar joint 3/8"
Filenames: concrete-slab-length-width-thickness-diagram.webp (if exported), alt: "Concrete slab diagram showing length, width, and thickness measurements" — descriptive, not stuffed, responsive, no CLS, WebP where possible, lazy below fold.
